========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, hi. My mom brought me. She... well, mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room. I think I've had these same clothes on for a few days. I used to knit a lot... I had this really soft purple yarn, it was supposed to be a blanket for my cousin's new baby. Babies are just so loud, though, you know? I don't really get why people want them. They just scream. 

But anyway, I don't knit anymore. I don't journal or go walking like I used to, either. I used to walk down by the creek and there are these rocks in the water that kind of look like faces if the sun hits them right. But now... I don't know. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong, I barely have the energy to even answer her. She made chicken last night and it was really dry, but I didn't even say anything because talking is just... it's just too much effort. So she brought me here.

Um... no. I don't think about dying. I just don't want to do anything. Being dead just means they put you in the ground anyway, and the dirt is always so wet and full of worms. Um... the date? I think it's... maybe the 14th? Or the 15th? I'm not really sure. Time is just kind of weird lately. Like, the days just blend together when you're in the same room all the time. He actually quit the band a few years ago to start a farm or something. Alpacas, I think. Alpacas are so funny looking, with those long necks and the fluffy hair on top of their heads. They always look like they're judging you. My aunt went to an alpaca farm once and bought this sweater made out of their wool, but she said it was so itchy she couldn't even wear it. Why spend all that money on a sweater you can't wear? Clothes are so expensive now anyway. I tried to buy a pair of jeans online, but the sizing is completely different depending on the brand. It's so stupid. But um... yeah, I don't really know what the date is. Sorry.

Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. Not really. I'm not a very energetic person. I usually sleep okay... well, except my bed gets really hot now. My mom bought this memory foam pad for it a while ago because she said my old mattress was sinking in the middle. But the foam just traps all your body heat. \n\nShe sprayed it with this lavender stuff that's supposed to make you sleepy, but it just smells like... like my grandma's bathroom. She used to have these little pink soaps shaped like seashells in a glass dish next to the sink. You weren't allowed to actually wash your hands with them, though. They were just for decoration. I always thought that was so stupid. Why put soap right next to the water if it's just going to sit there collecting dust? People do weird things with their houses. Like... those fuzzy covers people put on toilet lids? I don't get that at all. \n\nBut um... no. I don't get super energetic or stop sleeping.\n\nDoctor: When things feel like they are too much effort, have you ever had thoughts that you would rather not be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think about dying. I just don't want to do anything. Being dead just means they put you in the ground anyway, and the dirt is always so wet and full of worms. I saw this massive earthworm on our driveway after it rained a few days ago. It was just stretching out, trying to get across the concrete before the sun came out and dried it up. Birds always get them when they do that. Robins and pigeons. Pigeons are kind of gross, though. They have those shiny purple and green feathers on their necks, but their feet look exactly like little dinosaur claws. \n\nI watched this documentary once about how birds are actually related to dinosaurs. It's really weird to think that a T-Rex basically turned into a chicken. Especially since chicken is usually so dry... like the one my mom made for dinner the other night. You'd think a dinosaur would taste better than that. But um... no. I don't want to be dead. I just want to sit in my room.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, your mind tends to jump between many different topics very quickly; have you or anyone else noticed that your thinking or your memory has changed recently?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, I guess my mom said something about it the other day. She was complaining that I don't listen to her anymore, but she always talks when the TV is on. We were watching this baking show, the one where they have to make everything look like real objects? Like, they made a cake that looked exactly like a dirty sneaker. Why would you want to eat a sneaker? Even if it's cake, your brain just looks at it and thinks about feet. \n\nFeet are so weird if you actually look at them for too long. The toes are just like little stubby fingers that don't do anything. I stubbed my pinky toe on my desk chair last week, the one with the wheels that always get stuck on the carpet. It hurt so bad I thought I broke it, but it just turned this ugly purple color. Purple is my favorite color, though. Well, not that shade. More like a lavender. Like that spray my mom uses... wait. What were you saying about my memory? I don't know, I guess it's fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what the date is today?\n\nPatient: Um... the date? I think it's... maybe the 14th? Or the 15th? I'm not really sure. Time is just kind of weird lately. Like, the days just blend together when you're in the same room all the time. My room has these blinds that don't shut all the way, so there's always this one crack of light that hits the wall in the afternoon. It lines up perfectly with this poster I have... it's a band poster, but the lead singer's face is right where the light hits, so he just looks like he's glowing. \n\nHe actually quit the band a few years ago to start a farm or something. Alpacas, I think. Alpacas are so funny looking, with those long necks and the fluffy hair on top of their heads. They always look like they're judging you. My aunt went to an alpaca farm once and bought this sweater made out of their wool, but she said it was so itchy she couldn't even wear it. Why spend all that money on a sweater you can't wear? Clothes are so expensive now anyway. I tried to buy a pair of jeans online, but the sizing is completely different depending on the brand. It's so stupid. But um... yeah, I don't really know what the date is. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you have any trouble with your balance, coordination, or physical movements lately, or have you had any recent injuries like hitting your head?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't hit my head or anything. My balance is fine, I guess. I mean, I don't really walk around that much anyway. Just to the kitchen and back. We have this really old linoleum floor in the kitchen that's starting to peel up at the corners. It's this ugly yellow color, like mustard that's been sitting out too long. I hate mustard. It's too tangy. People put it on hot dogs, but hot dogs are basically just mystery meat anyway. \n\nI read somewhere that they put lips and snouts in hot dogs. Or maybe that was bologna? Bologna is so weird. It's perfectly round. Nothing in nature is perfectly round like that. Even oranges have those little bumps on them. My mom bought this bag of oranges last week, but they were super sour. I tried to eat one while I was sitting on the floor in my room, but the juice got on my hands and made them all sticky. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Just the pipes.\n\n[Last Question]\nI've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 19 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. 

Sarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.

Bologna is so weird. It's perfectly round. Nothing in nature is perfectly round like that. Even oranges have those little bumps on them. My mom bought this bag of oranges last week, but they were super sour. I tried to eat one while I was sitting on the floor in my room, but the juice got on my hands and made them all sticky. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\nPatient: Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. \n\nSarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like you have special or unusual connections to things around you, or that you can sense things happening that others might not be aware of?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... special connections? I mean, I don't know if it's a special connection, but I definitely notice things that other people just completely ignore. Like, when I was taking those college classes before I stopped going, I realized what they were doing. The school portal and the clinic practicum people, they were using these little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments. It was so obvious to me. They were marking me, like, signaling to each other that they didn't want me in healthcare. 

My mom says they're just regular messages, but she doesn't read them the way I do. She reads everything so fast. She skims the newspaper every morning while she drinks her coffee. She always drinks it out of this chipped blue mug that says "World's Okayest Mom." My brother got it for her for Mother's Day a few years ago. He thought it was hilarious, but I think it's kind of mean. Mother's Day is such a weird holiday anyway. It's basically just invented by greeting card companies to sell overpriced pieces of paper with glitter on them. Glitter is the absolute worst. If you get it on your hands, it stays there for weeks. It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. But yeah, I know what those emails really meant, even if no one else sees it.

I don't get super energetic or stop sleeping.\n\nDoctor: When things feel like they are too much effort, have you ever had thoughts that you would rather not be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think about dying. I just don't want to do anything. Being dead just means they put you in the ground anyway, and the dirt is always so wet and full of worms. I saw this massive earthworm on our driveway after it rained a few days ago. It was just stretching out, trying to get across the concrete before the sun came out and dried it up. Birds always get them when they do that. Robins and pigeons. Pigeons are kind of gross, though. They have those shiny purple and green feathers on their necks, but their feet look exactly like little dinosaur claws. \n\nI watched this documentary once about how birds are actually related to dinosaurs. It's really weird to think that a T-Rex basically turned into a chicken. Especially since chicken is usually so dry... like the one my mom made for dinner the other night. You'd think a dinosaur would taste better than that. But um... no. I don't want to be dead. I just want to sit in my room.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, your mind tends to jump between many different topics very quickly; have you or anyone else noticed that your thinking or your memory has changed recently?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, I guess my mom said something about it the other day. She was complaining that I don't listen to her anymore, but she always talks when the TV is on. We were watching this baking show, the one where they have to make everything look like real objects? Like, they made a cake that looked exactly like a dirty sneaker. Why would you want to eat a sneaker? Even if it's cake, your brain just looks at it and thinks about feet. \n\nFeet are so weird if you actually look at them for too long. The toes are just like little stubby fingers that don't do anything. I stubbed my pinky toe on my desk chair last week, the one with the wheels that always get stuck on the carpet. It hurt so bad I thought I broke it, but it just turned this ugly purple color. Purple is my favorite color, though. Well, not that shade. More like a lavender. Like that spray my mom uses... wait. What were you saying about my memory? I don't know, I guess it's fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what the date is today?\n\nPatient: Um... the date? I think it's... maybe the 14th? Or the 15th? I'm not really sure. Time is just kind of weird lately. Like, the days just blend together when you're in the same room all the time. My room has these blinds that don't shut all the way, so there's always this one crack of light that hits the wall in the afternoon. It lines up perfectly with this poster I have... it's a band poster, but the lead singer's face is right where the light hits, so he just looks like he's glowing. \n\nHe actually quit the band a few years ago to start a farm or something. Alpacas, I think. Alpacas are so funny looking, with those long necks and the fluffy hair on top of their heads. They always look like they're judging you. My aunt went to an alpaca farm once and bought this sweater made out of their wool, but she said it was so itchy she couldn't even wear it. Why spend all that money on a sweater you can't wear? Clothes are so expensive now anyway. I tried to buy a pair of jeans online, but the sizing is completely different depending on the brand. It's so stupid. But um... yeah, I don't really know what the date is. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you have any trouble with your balance, coordination, or physical movements lately, or have you had any recent injuries like hitting your head?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't hit my head or anything. My balance is fine, I guess. I mean, I don't really walk around that much anyway. Just to the kitchen and back. We have this really old linoleum floor in the kitchen that's starting to peel up at the corners. It's this ugly yellow color, like mustard that's been sitting out too long. I hate mustard. It's too tangy. People put it on hot dogs, but hot dogs are basically just mystery meat anyway. \n\nI read somewhere that they put lips and snouts in hot dogs. Or maybe that was bologna? Bologna is so weird. It's perfectly round. Nothing in nature is perfectly round like that. Even oranges have those little bumps on them. My mom bought this bag of oranges last week, but they were super sour. I tried to eat one while I was sitting on the floor in my room, but the juice got on my hands and made them all sticky. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\nPatient: Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. \n\nSarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special or unusual connections to things around you, or that you can sense things happening that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Um... special connections? I mean, I don't know if it's a special connection, but I definitely notice things that other people just completely ignore. Like, when I was taking those college classes before I stopped going, I realized what they were doing. The school portal and the clinic practicum people, they were using these little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments. It was so obvious to me. They were marking me, like, signaling to each other that they didn't want me in healthcare. \n\nMy mom says they're just regular messages, but she doesn't read them the way I do. She reads everything so fast. She skims the newspaper every morning while she drinks her coffee. She always drinks it out of this chipped blue mug that says \"World's Okayest Mom.\" My brother got it for her for Mother's Day a few years ago. He thought it was hilarious, but I think it's kind of mean. Mother's Day is such a weird holiday anyway. It's basically just invented by greeting card companies to sell overpriced pieces of paper with glitter on them. Glitter is the absolute worst. If you get it on your hands, it stays there for weeks. It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. Harm me? Well, yeah, they are definitely interfering. Like I said, since I was in those college classes, I've just known that the school portal and the clinic practicum people were doing it on purpose. They use those little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments to mark me, to make sure I'm flagged as someone they don't want in healthcare. My mom keeps saying they're just regular messages, but she doesn't get it. 

She doesn't get a lot of things, honestly. Like how she always buys the wrong kind of bread at the grocery store. She gets that heavy wheat bread with all the tiny seeds baked right into the crust. I hate seeds in bread. They get stuck in your teeth and you can't get them out. I had to go to the dentist last year because a popcorn kernel got stuck in my gums for a whole week, and the dentist had to use this sharp little metal hook thing to scrape it out. Dentists are so terrifying. They always ask you questions about your life when both of their hands are literally inside your mouth, which makes zero sense. How are you supposed to answer them? I just kind of grunt. 

My uncle grunts a lot when he watches football on Sundays. He gets so mad at the referees on the TV. He threw a couch cushion at the screen once and knocked over a lamp in the living room. It was a really ugly lamp anyway, it had this weird beaded fringe hanging off the shade. Fringe is so outdated, it looks like something from an old cowboy movie. I don't really like cowboy movies, there's always too much dirt, and horses kind of scare me. Their teeth are way too big, and they look like they're always about to bite you. But um... yeah, they're interfering with my life for sure.

She used to have these little pink soaps shaped like seashells in a glass dish next to the sink. You weren't allowed to actually wash your hands with them, though. They were just for decoration. I always thought that was so stupid. Why put soap right next to the water if it's just going to sit there collecting dust? People do weird things with their houses. Like... those fuzzy covers people put on toilet lids? I don't get that at all. \n\nBut um... no. I don't get super energetic or stop sleeping.\n\nDoctor: When things feel like they are too much effort, have you ever had thoughts that you would rather not be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think about dying. I just don't want to do anything. Being dead just means they put you in the ground anyway, and the dirt is always so wet and full of worms. I saw this massive earthworm on our driveway after it rained a few days ago. It was just stretching out, trying to get across the concrete before the sun came out and dried it up. Birds always get them when they do that. Robins and pigeons. Pigeons are kind of gross, though. They have those shiny purple and green feathers on their necks, but their feet look exactly like little dinosaur claws. \n\nI watched this documentary once about how birds are actually related to dinosaurs. It's really weird to think that a T-Rex basically turned into a chicken. Especially since chicken is usually so dry... like the one my mom made for dinner the other night. You'd think a dinosaur would taste better than that. But um... no. I don't want to be dead. I just want to sit in my room.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, your mind tends to jump between many different topics very quickly; have you or anyone else noticed that your thinking or your memory has changed recently?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, I guess my mom said something about it the other day. She was complaining that I don't listen to her anymore, but she always talks when the TV is on. We were watching this baking show, the one where they have to make everything look like real objects? Like, they made a cake that looked exactly like a dirty sneaker. Why would you want to eat a sneaker? Even if it's cake, your brain just looks at it and thinks about feet. \n\nFeet are so weird if you actually look at them for too long. The toes are just like little stubby fingers that don't do anything. I stubbed my pinky toe on my desk chair last week, the one with the wheels that always get stuck on the carpet. It hurt so bad I thought I broke it, but it just turned this ugly purple color. Purple is my favorite color, though. Well, not that shade. More like a lavender. Like that spray my mom uses... wait. What were you saying about my memory? I don't know, I guess it's fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what the date is today?\n\nPatient: Um... the date? I think it's... maybe the 14th? Or the 15th? I'm not really sure. Time is just kind of weird lately. Like, the days just blend together when you're in the same room all the time. My room has these blinds that don't shut all the way, so there's always this one crack of light that hits the wall in the afternoon. It lines up perfectly with this poster I have... it's a band poster, but the lead singer's face is right where the light hits, so he just looks like he's glowing. \n\nHe actually quit the band a few years ago to start a farm or something. Alpacas, I think. Alpacas are so funny looking, with those long necks and the fluffy hair on top of their heads. They always look like they're judging you. My aunt went to an alpaca farm once and bought this sweater made out of their wool, but she said it was so itchy she couldn't even wear it. Why spend all that money on a sweater you can't wear? Clothes are so expensive now anyway. I tried to buy a pair of jeans online, but the sizing is completely different depending on the brand. It's so stupid. But um... yeah, I don't really know what the date is. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you have any trouble with your balance, coordination, or physical movements lately, or have you had any recent injuries like hitting your head?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't hit my head or anything. My balance is fine, I guess. I mean, I don't really walk around that much anyway. Just to the kitchen and back. We have this really old linoleum floor in the kitchen that's starting to peel up at the corners. It's this ugly yellow color, like mustard that's been sitting out too long. I hate mustard. It's too tangy. People put it on hot dogs, but hot dogs are basically just mystery meat anyway. \n\nI read somewhere that they put lips and snouts in hot dogs. Or maybe that was bologna? Bologna is so weird. It's perfectly round. Nothing in nature is perfectly round like that. Even oranges have those little bumps on them. My mom bought this bag of oranges last week, but they were super sour. I tried to eat one while I was sitting on the floor in my room, but the juice got on my hands and made them all sticky. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\nPatient: Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. \n\nSarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special or unusual connections to things around you, or that you can sense things happening that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Um... special connections? I mean, I don't know if it's a special connection, but I definitely notice things that other people just completely ignore. Like, when I was taking those college classes before I stopped going, I realized what they were doing. The school portal and the clinic practicum people, they were using these little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments. It was so obvious to me. They were marking me, like, signaling to each other that they didn't want me in healthcare. \n\nMy mom says they're just regular messages, but she doesn't read them the way I do. She reads everything so fast. She skims the newspaper every morning while she drinks her coffee. She always drinks it out of this chipped blue mug that says \"World's Okayest Mom.\" My brother got it for her for Mother's Day a few years ago. He thought it was hilarious, but I think it's kind of mean. Mother's Day is such a weird holiday anyway. It's basically just invented by greeting card companies to sell overpriced pieces of paper with glitter on them. Glitter is the absolute worst. If you get it on your hands, it stays there for weeks. It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. But yeah, I know what those emails really meant, even if no one else sees it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that the emails from your school and clinic were signaling that they didn't want you in healthcare; do you feel like these people are actively trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Well, yeah, they are definitely interfering. Like I said, since I was in those college classes, I've just known that the school portal and the clinic practicum people were doing it on purpose. They use those little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments to mark me, to make sure I'm flagged as someone they don't want in healthcare. My mom keeps saying they're just regular messages, but she doesn't get it. \n\nShe doesn't get a lot of things, honestly. Like how she always buys the wrong kind of bread at the grocery store. She gets that heavy wheat bread with all the tiny seeds baked right into the crust. I hate seeds in bread. They get stuck in your teeth and you can't get them out. I had to go to the dentist last year because a popcorn kernel got stuck in my gums for a whole week, and the dentist had to use this sharp little metal hook thing to scrape it out. Dentists are so terrifying. They always ask you questions about your life when both of their hands are literally inside your mouth, which makes zero sense. How are you supposed to answer them? I just kind of grunt. \n\nMy uncle grunts a lot when he watches football on Sundays. He gets so mad at the referees on the TV. He threw a couch cushion at the screen once and knocked over a lamp in the living room. It was a really ugly lamp anyway, it had this weird beaded fringe hanging off the shade. Fringe is so outdated, it looks like something from an old cowboy movie. I don't really like cowboy movies, there's always too much dirt, and horses kind of scare me. Their teeth are way too big, and they look like they're always about to bite you. But um... yeah, they're interfering with my life for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I read somewhere that birds are actually related to dinosaurs. Pigeons are basically tiny dinosaurs. There are so many pigeons downtown, they just walk around like they own the sidewalks. Some guy was feeding them bread crusts the other day, and they were all swarming him. It looked like a horror movie. Have you ever seen that old movie with the birds? My dad loves old movies. He has a whole collection of DVDs on a shelf in the basement. The basement always smells kind of damp, like wet cardboard. We had a flood down there a few years ago when the water heater broke, and we had to throw away so much stuff. It was a huge mess. So, um, no, I don't really have trouble getting dressed or making food. I just eat whatever is around.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you are trying to do things like finish a project or manage your daily schedule, do you find that your thoughts become so jumbled or scattered that you simply cannot complete the task?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 19 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... finish a project? I don't really have projects anymore since I stopped going to classes. But my schedule... I mean, I don't know about scattered. I just kind of do whatever. Sometimes I sit in the living room and try to watch TV, but the commercials are so loud. Why do they make the commercials louder than the actual show? It's like they're yelling at you to buy things. 

My mom bought this new vacuum cleaner from an infomercial she saw late at night. It's supposed to pick up pet hair, even though we don't even have a pet. I wanted a dog when I was little, a golden retriever, but my dad said he was allergic. I don't think he was actually allergic, I think he just didn't want to walk it in the winter. Winter here is awful, the snow gets all gray and slushy on the sides of the road, and the salt ruins your shoes. I had these really nice black boots last year, and the salt left these white crusty lines all over them. I tried wiping them with a wet cloth, but it just made it worse. They were suede, and you're not supposed to get suede wet. It's such a high-maintenance material. Kind of like velvet. Velvet feels weird if you rub it the wrong way, it gives me the shivers. So, yeah, I don't really know about my thoughts being jumbled for a schedule, I just don't have much to do anyway.

It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. They use those little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments to mark me, to make sure I'm flagged as someone they don't want in healthcare. My mom keeps saying they're just regular messages, but she doesn't get it. \n\nShe doesn't get a lot of things, honestly. Like how she always buys the wrong kind of bread at the grocery store. She gets that heavy wheat bread with all the tiny seeds baked right into the crust. I hate seeds in bread. They get stuck in your teeth and you can't get them out. I had to go to the dentist last year because a popcorn kernel got stuck in my gums for a whole week, and the dentist had to use this sharp little metal hook thing to scrape it out. Dentists are so terrifying. They always ask you questions about your life when both of their hands are literally inside your mouth, which makes zero sense. How are you supposed to answer them? I just kind of grunt. \n\nMy uncle grunts a lot when he watches football on Sundays. He gets so mad at the referees on the TV. He threw a couch cushion at the screen once and knocked over a lamp in the living room. It was a really ugly lamp anyway, it had this weird beaded fringe hanging off the shade. Fringe is so outdated, it looks like something from an old cowboy movie. I don't really like cowboy movies, there's always too much dirt, and horses kind of scare me. Their teeth are way too big, and they look like they're always about to bite you. But um... yeah, they're interfering with my life for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you are not thinking about those emails from school, do you ever find that your thoughts become so disorganized or jumbled that you have trouble carrying out basic daily tasks, like getting dressed or preparing a simple meal?\n\nPatient: Um... disorganized thoughts? I don't know if I'd call it disorganized. I mean, getting dressed is fine, mostly. I usually just grab whatever is on the chair. My mom hates when I leave clothes on the chair, she says it looks messy. But it's my room, so I don't see why it matters. She has this whole thing about keeping the house perfect, like it's a museum or something. \n\nWe went to a museum once on a field trip in middle school. It was the natural history one, with all the dinosaur bones. They had this huge T-Rex skeleton right in the lobby. It's crazy to think things that big used to walk around. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\nPatient: Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. \n\nSarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special or unusual connections to things around you, or that you can sense things happening that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Um... special connections? I mean, I don't know if it's a special connection, but I definitely notice things that other people just completely ignore. Like, when I was taking those college classes before I stopped going, I realized what they were doing. The school portal and the clinic practicum people, they were using these little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments. It was so obvious to me. They were marking me, like, signaling to each other that they didn't want me in healthcare. \n\nMy mom says they're just regular messages, but she doesn't read them the way I do. She reads everything so fast. She skims the newspaper every morning while she drinks her coffee. She always drinks it out of this chipped blue mug that says \"World's Okayest Mom.\" My brother got it for her for Mother's Day a few years ago. He thought it was hilarious, but I think it's kind of mean. Mother's Day is such a weird holiday anyway. It's basically just invented by greeting card companies to sell overpriced pieces of paper with glitter on them. Glitter is the absolute worst. If you get it on your hands, it stays there for weeks. It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. But yeah, I know what those emails really meant, even if no one else sees it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that the emails from your school and clinic were signaling that they didn't want you in healthcare; do you feel like these people are actively trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Well, yeah, they are definitely interfering. Like I said, since I was in those college classes, I've just known that the school portal and the clinic practicum people were doing it on purpose. They use those little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments to mark me, to make sure I'm flagged as someone they don't want in healthcare. My mom keeps saying they're just regular messages, but she doesn't get it. \n\nShe doesn't get a lot of things, honestly. Like how she always buys the wrong kind of bread at the grocery store. She gets that heavy wheat bread with all the tiny seeds baked right into the crust. I hate seeds in bread. They get stuck in your teeth and you can't get them out. I had to go to the dentist last year because a popcorn kernel got stuck in my gums for a whole week, and the dentist had to use this sharp little metal hook thing to scrape it out. Dentists are so terrifying. They always ask you questions about your life when both of their hands are literally inside your mouth, which makes zero sense. How are you supposed to answer them? I just kind of grunt. \n\nMy uncle grunts a lot when he watches football on Sundays. He gets so mad at the referees on the TV. He threw a couch cushion at the screen once and knocked over a lamp in the living room. It was a really ugly lamp anyway, it had this weird beaded fringe hanging off the shade. Fringe is so outdated, it looks like something from an old cowboy movie. I don't really like cowboy movies, there's always too much dirt, and horses kind of scare me. Their teeth are way too big, and they look like they're always about to bite you. But um... yeah, they're interfering with my life for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you are not thinking about those emails from school, do you ever find that your thoughts become so disorganized or jumbled that you have trouble carrying out basic daily tasks, like getting dressed or preparing a simple meal?\n\nPatient: Um... disorganized thoughts? I don't know if I'd call it disorganized. I mean, getting dressed is fine, mostly. I usually just grab whatever is on the chair. My mom hates when I leave clothes on the chair, she says it looks messy. But it's my room, so I don't see why it matters. She has this whole thing about keeping the house perfect, like it's a museum or something. \n\nWe went to a museum once on a field trip in middle school. It was the natural history one, with all the dinosaur bones. They had this huge T-Rex skeleton right in the lobby. It's crazy to think things that big used to walk around. I read somewhere that birds are actually related to dinosaurs. Pigeons are basically tiny dinosaurs. There are so many pigeons downtown, they just walk around like they own the sidewalks. Some guy was feeding them bread crusts the other day, and they were all swarming him. It looked like a horror movie. Have you ever seen that old movie with the birds? My dad loves old movies. He has a whole collection of DVDs on a shelf in the basement. The basement always smells kind of damp, like wet cardboard. We had a flood down there a few years ago when the water heater broke, and we had to throw away so much stuff. It was a huge mess. So, um, no, I don't really have trouble getting dressed or making food. I just eat whatever is around.\n\nDoctor: When you are trying to do things like finish a project or manage your daily schedule, do you find that your thoughts become so jumbled or scattered that you simply cannot complete the task?\n\nPatient: Um... finish a project? I don't really have projects anymore since I stopped going to classes. But my schedule... I mean, I don't know about scattered. I just kind of do whatever. Sometimes I sit in the living room and try to watch TV, but the commercials are so loud. Why do they make the commercials louder than the actual show? It's like they're yelling at you to buy things. \n\nMy mom bought this new vacuum cleaner from an infomercial she saw late at night. It's supposed to pick up pet hair, even though we don't even have a pet. I wanted a dog when I was little, a golden retriever, but my dad said he was allergic. I don't think he was actually allergic, I think he just didn't want to walk it in the winter. Winter here is awful, the snow gets all gray and slushy on the sides of the road, and the salt ruins your shoes. I had these really nice black boots last year, and the salt left these white crusty lines all over them. I tried wiping them with a wet cloth, but it just made it worse. They were suede, and you're not supposed to get suede wet. It's such a high-maintenance material. Kind of like velvet. Velvet feels weird if you rub it the wrong way, it gives me the shivers. So, yeah, I don't really know about my thoughts being jumbled for a schedule, I just don't have much to do anyway.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um... how long? Well, I mean, this whole decline, like, everything getting weird... it's been going on since I started the community college program. That was about two years and two months ago, I think. That's when I first noticed the wording in the emails. But community college is weird anyway. The campus was so spread out, and the parking lot was always full. I had to park in this dirt lot all the way in the back, and when it rained, it turned into this massive mud pit. 

Mud is so gross, it gets stuck in the treads of your sneakers and you end up tracking it everywhere. My mom yelled at me once because I tracked mud into the kitchen right after she mopped. She uses this lemon-scented floor cleaner, but it doesn't even smell like real lemons. It smells like those cheap yellow hard candies that old people always keep in a glass dish on their coffee table. My grandma had a dish like that, but it was shaped like a swan. Swans are actually really mean birds. People think they're graceful and romantic, but if you get too close to them, they'll hiss at you and chase you. I saw a video online of a swan chasing a guy on a golf course. Golf seems like such a boring sport anyway. You just walk around hitting a tiny ball with a stick. I don't understand why people watch it on TV, it's basically just whispering and grass. So yeah, about two years and two months ago is when it all started.

No, I've never just sat around like this before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt unusually energetic, talkative, or unable to sleep, even if you felt great at the time?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I'm not a very energetic person. I usually sleep okay... well, except my bed gets really hot now. My mom bought this memory foam pad for it a while ago because she said my old mattress was sinking in the middle. But the foam just traps all your body heat. \n\nShe sprayed it with this lavender stuff that's supposed to make you sleepy, but it just smells like... like my grandma's bathroom. She used to have these little pink soaps shaped like seashells in a glass dish next to the sink. You weren't allowed to actually wash your hands with them, though. They were just for decoration. I always thought that was so stupid. Why put soap right next to the water if it's just going to sit there collecting dust? People do weird things with their houses. Like... those fuzzy covers people put on toilet lids? I don't get that at all. \n\nBut um... no. I don't get super energetic or stop sleeping.\n\nDoctor: When things feel like they are too much effort, have you ever had thoughts that you would rather not be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think about dying. I just don't want to do anything. Being dead just means they put you in the ground anyway, and the dirt is always so wet and full of worms. I saw this massive earthworm on our driveway after it rained a few days ago. It was just stretching out, trying to get across the concrete before the sun came out and dried it up. Birds always get them when they do that. Robins and pigeons. Pigeons are kind of gross, though. They have those shiny purple and green feathers on their necks, but their feet look exactly like little dinosaur claws. \n\nI watched this documentary once about how birds are actually related to dinosaurs. It's really weird to think that a T-Rex basically turned into a chicken. Especially since chicken is usually so dry... like the one my mom made for dinner the other night. You'd think a dinosaur would taste better than that. But um... no. I don't want to be dead. I just want to sit in my room.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, your mind tends to jump between many different topics very quickly; have you or anyone else noticed that your thinking or your memory has changed recently?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, I guess my mom said something about it the other day. She was complaining that I don't listen to her anymore, but she always talks when the TV is on. We were watching this baking show, the one where they have to make everything look like real objects? Like, they made a cake that looked exactly like a dirty sneaker. Why would you want to eat a sneaker? Even if it's cake, your brain just looks at it and thinks about feet. \n\nFeet are so weird if you actually look at them for too long. The toes are just like little stubby fingers that don't do anything. I stubbed my pinky toe on my desk chair last week, the one with the wheels that always get stuck on the carpet. It hurt so bad I thought I broke it, but it just turned this ugly purple color. Purple is my favorite color, though. Well, not that shade. More like a lavender. Like that spray my mom uses... wait. What were you saying about my memory? I don't know, I guess it's fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what the date is today?\n\nPatient: Um... the date? I think it's... maybe the 14th? Or the 15th? I'm not really sure. Time is just kind of weird lately. Like, the days just blend together when you're in the same room all the time. My room has these blinds that don't shut all the way, so there's always this one crack of light that hits the wall in the afternoon. It lines up perfectly with this poster I have... it's a band poster, but the lead singer's face is right where the light hits, so he just looks like he's glowing. \n\nHe actually quit the band a few years ago to start a farm or something. Alpacas, I think. Alpacas are so funny looking, with those long necks and the fluffy hair on top of their heads. They always look like they're judging you. My aunt went to an alpaca farm once and bought this sweater made out of their wool, but she said it was so itchy she couldn't even wear it. Why spend all that money on a sweater you can't wear? Clothes are so expensive now anyway. I tried to buy a pair of jeans online, but the sizing is completely different depending on the brand. It's so stupid. But um... yeah, I don't really know what the date is. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if you have any trouble with your balance, coordination, or physical movements lately, or have you had any recent injuries like hitting your head?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't hit my head or anything. My balance is fine, I guess. I mean, I don't really walk around that much anyway. Just to the kitchen and back. We have this really old linoleum floor in the kitchen that's starting to peel up at the corners. It's this ugly yellow color, like mustard that's been sitting out too long. I hate mustard. It's too tangy. People put it on hot dogs, but hot dogs are basically just mystery meat anyway. \n\nI read somewhere that they put lips and snouts in hot dogs. Or maybe that was bologna? Bologna is so weird. It's perfectly round. Nothing in nature is perfectly round like that. Even oranges have those little bumps on them. My mom bought this bag of oranges last week, but they were super sour. I tried to eat one while I was sitting on the floor in my room, but the juice got on my hands and made them all sticky. I hate when my hands are sticky. It makes everything you touch feel gross. Like when you touch a doorknob at a public place and you just know a hundred other people touched it. But um... no, I haven't fallen or hurt my head.\n\nDoctor: When you are alone in your room, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? No, I don't... I don't hear anything like that. Just the regular stuff, you know? Like the pipes in the walls. They make this weird groaning noise sometimes, especially at night when the house gets really quiet. It sounds like someone is dragging a heavy box across the floor upstairs, but there's no upstairs. We just have an attic, and the only thing up there is a bunch of old holiday decorations. \n\nMy mom refuses to throw away this one plastic light-up Santa from like, 1995. The bulb inside it burned out years ago, but she says it's nostalgic. Nostalgia is weird, isn't it? People get so attached to junk just because they had it when they were younger. Like my old phone. I still have it in a drawer even though the screen is completely shattered. I dropped it on the driveway when I was trying to carry in groceries. The bags were so heavy, and the handles were digging into my wrists. I hate those thin plastic bags. They always rip if you put anything with corners in them, like cereal boxes. Cereal is getting so expensive, too. A box of the good kind with the marshmallows is like six dollars now. It's ridiculous. But um... no, I don't hear voices or see things. Just the pipes.\n\nDoctor: I've noticed that when we talk, you often start on one topic and then move to a completely different one; does this happen to you when you are by yourself, or only when you are talking to other people?\n\nPatient: Um... moving to different topics? I don't really know. I guess I just think about a lot of things at once. Even when I'm alone, my brain just kind of... goes places. Like, I'll be sitting on my bed looking at the ceiling, and the ceiling has that popcorn texture, you know? It looks like cottage cheese. I tried eating cottage cheese once because my friend Sarah said it was healthy, but the texture is so gross. It's like lumpy milk. \n\nSarah used to be obsessed with all those health food trends. She bought this huge tub of protein powder that smelled like fake vanilla, and she'd mix it into everything. She even tried making pancakes with it, but they turned out like rubber disks. We tried to feed one to her dog, Buster, but he wouldn't even eat it. Buster was a golden retriever, but he was really lazy. He'd just lay in the middle of the hallway so you had to step over him. I tripped over him once and spilled a whole glass of water. It soaked right into the carpet, and it took forever to dry. Wet carpet smells terrible, kind of like old towels that got left in the washing machine too long. You have to re-wash them with vinegar to get the smell out. Vinegar fixes almost everything, honestly. My mom uses it to clean the windows, but it makes the whole house smell like a salad dressing factory. But um... yeah, I don't know if I do it when I'm alone. I just think about stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special or unusual connections to things around you, or that you can sense things happening that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Um... special connections? I mean, I don't know if it's a special connection, but I definitely notice things that other people just completely ignore. Like, when I was taking those college classes before I stopped going, I realized what they were doing. The school portal and the clinic practicum people, they were using these little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments. It was so obvious to me. They were marking me, like, signaling to each other that they didn't want me in healthcare. \n\nMy mom says they're just regular messages, but she doesn't read them the way I do. She reads everything so fast. She skims the newspaper every morning while she drinks her coffee. She always drinks it out of this chipped blue mug that says \"World's Okayest Mom.\" My brother got it for her for Mother's Day a few years ago. He thought it was hilarious, but I think it's kind of mean. Mother's Day is such a weird holiday anyway. It's basically just invented by greeting card companies to sell overpriced pieces of paper with glitter on them. Glitter is the absolute worst. If you get it on your hands, it stays there for weeks. It's like a virus. My friend had a glitter-themed birthday party in middle school, and I'm pretty sure there's still glitter in the backseat of my mom's car from when she drove us home. The car is this old silver Honda, and the air conditioning doesn't even work right anymore. It just blows warm air that smells like dust. But yeah, I know what those emails really meant, even if no one else sees it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that the emails from your school and clinic were signaling that they didn't want you in healthcare; do you feel like these people are actively trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Well, yeah, they are definitely interfering. Like I said, since I was in those college classes, I've just known that the school portal and the clinic practicum people were doing it on purpose. They use those little wording changes in the emails and the assignment comments to mark me, to make sure I'm flagged as someone they don't want in healthcare. My mom keeps saying they're just regular messages, but she doesn't get it. \n\nShe doesn't get a lot of things, honestly. Like how she always buys the wrong kind of bread at the grocery store. She gets that heavy wheat bread with all the tiny seeds baked right into the crust. I hate seeds in bread. They get stuck in your teeth and you can't get them out. I had to go to the dentist last year because a popcorn kernel got stuck in my gums for a whole week, and the dentist had to use this sharp little metal hook thing to scrape it out. Dentists are so terrifying. They always ask you questions about your life when both of their hands are literally inside your mouth, which makes zero sense. How are you supposed to answer them? I just kind of grunt. \n\nMy uncle grunts a lot when he watches football on Sundays. He gets so mad at the referees on the TV. He threw a couch cushion at the screen once and knocked over a lamp in the living room. It was a really ugly lamp anyway, it had this weird beaded fringe hanging off the shade. Fringe is so outdated, it looks like something from an old cowboy movie. I don't really like cowboy movies, there's always too much dirt, and horses kind of scare me. Their teeth are way too big, and they look like they're always about to bite you. But um... yeah, they're interfering with my life for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you are not thinking about those emails from school, do you ever find that your thoughts become so disorganized or jumbled that you have trouble carrying out basic daily tasks, like getting dressed or preparing a simple meal?\n\nPatient: Um... disorganized thoughts? I don't know if I'd call it disorganized. I mean, getting dressed is fine, mostly. I usually just grab whatever is on the chair. My mom hates when I leave clothes on the chair, she says it looks messy. But it's my room, so I don't see why it matters. She has this whole thing about keeping the house perfect, like it's a museum or something. \n\nWe went to a museum once on a field trip in middle school. It was the natural history one, with all the dinosaur bones. They had this huge T-Rex skeleton right in the lobby. It's crazy to think things that big used to walk around. I read somewhere that birds are actually related to dinosaurs. Pigeons are basically tiny dinosaurs. There are so many pigeons downtown, they just walk around like they own the sidewalks. Some guy was feeding them bread crusts the other day, and they were all swarming him. It looked like a horror movie. Have you ever seen that old movie with the birds? My dad loves old movies. He has a whole collection of DVDs on a shelf in the basement. The basement always smells kind of damp, like wet cardboard. We had a flood down there a few years ago when the water heater broke, and we had to throw away so much stuff. It was a huge mess. So, um, no, I don't really have trouble getting dressed or making food. I just eat whatever is around.\n\nDoctor: When you are trying to do things like finish a project or manage your daily schedule, do you find that your thoughts become so jumbled or scattered that you simply cannot complete the task?\n\nPatient: Um... finish a project? I don't really have projects anymore since I stopped going to classes. But my schedule... I mean, I don't know about scattered. I just kind of do whatever. Sometimes I sit in the living room and try to watch TV, but the commercials are so loud. Why do they make the commercials louder than the actual show? It's like they're yelling at you to buy things. \n\nMy mom bought this new vacuum cleaner from an infomercial she saw late at night. It's supposed to pick up pet hair, even though we don't even have a pet. I wanted a dog when I was little, a golden retriever, but my dad said he was allergic. I don't think he was actually allergic, I think he just didn't want to walk it in the winter. Winter here is awful, the snow gets all gray and slushy on the sides of the road, and the salt ruins your shoes. I had these really nice black boots last year, and the salt left these white crusty lines all over them. I tried wiping them with a wet cloth, but it just made it worse. They were suede, and you're not supposed to get suede wet. It's such a high-maintenance material. Kind of like velvet. Velvet feels weird if you rub it the wrong way, it gives me the shivers. So, yeah, I don't really know about my thoughts being jumbled for a schedule, I just don't have much to do anyway.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you finished high school last year; how long have you felt that the school and clinic were sending you these messages and that you've been struggling with your motivation?\n\nPatient: Um... how long? Well, I mean, this whole decline, like, everything getting weird... it's been going on since I started the community college program. That was about two years and two months ago, I think. That's when I first noticed the wording in the emails. But community college is weird anyway. The campus was so spread out, and the parking lot was always full. I had to park in this dirt lot all the way in the back, and when it rained, it turned into this massive mud pit. \n\nMud is so gross, it gets stuck in the treads of your sneakers and you end up tracking it everywhere. My mom yelled at me once because I tracked mud into the kitchen right after she mopped. She uses this lemon-scented floor cleaner, but it doesn't even smell like real lemons. It smells like those cheap yellow hard candies that old people always keep in a glass dish on their coffee table. My grandma had a dish like that, but it was shaped like a swan. Swans are actually really mean birds. People think they're graceful and romantic, but if you get too close to them, they'll hiss at you and chase you. I saw a video online of a swan chasing a guy on a golf course. Golf seems like such a boring sport anyway. You just walk around hitting a tiny ball with a stick. I don't understand why people watch it on TV, it's basically just whispering and grass. So yeah, about two years and two months ago is when it all started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
